The situation in Austria is thus relatively better, and this is a result of the agricultural policy actions. One point was that we had overproduction due to the collapse of the markets in China – armored milk – which were suddenly gone for the European market. Due to the high milk price in 2007, milk in the industry was displaced from the recipe – in the case of ice cream, in the production of biscuits – so there was an oversupply. That is why the strategy of the Austrian agricultural policy was: Clear milk market. This is what we have done at European level, through export refunds, through intervention of milk, in order to balance supply and demand. There has always been structural change in agriculture and will continue to exist in the future. To deny this would be a pretense of false facts. The fact is that structural change has slowed down, including through our policies. We have had a structural change in the period before EU accession of 5 to 6 percent and have now slowed down a structural change of 2 to 3 percent, especially in difficult times. This is the result of our agricultural policy, which is successful here. Next point: climate change. Brunner.]] We do not achieve Kyoto targets in transport, industry and waste heat. That is to say, agriculture is doing its best. Because you always highlight the biointroduction stop here and present it as if the organic farmers were no longer promoting: The fact is that there is an entry-level stop for both conventional and organic farmers, because the period is just ending in 2013, but farmers are committed to a five-year commitment and there must be a guarantee. Every farmer understands that. If you're talking to a farmer in practice, he says: Yes. But it can now very well be a organic farmer, if he buys areas from conventional farmers, leases them, organically manages them, and he also receives a premium for that. An extension of the area is guaranteed, and it is about increasing the level of organic farming, with the importance that this is associated with the market, because it makes no sense for all to suddenly become organic farmers if something else is asked on the market. We currently have a market share of 6 to 7 percent for organic products, and 93 percent of the population is conventional, and this must also be respected. It is therefore also important that we have a competitive, innovative conventional agriculture in the same way to cover the table of the domestic population. That's our big concern. We don't want to use genetic engineering. In the meantime, the Commission has also joined this view, it has to dress in legal circles, but the path of the Austrian agricultural policy is one that is free of genetic engineering. We also carry this idea to Europe. We have received a lot of approval from many people and get new partners there. We will not give up our efforts at European level because I see this as a great opportunity for Austria. Finally, we have discussed this several times in this House – as it continues after 2013. It is clear that after 2013, we will continue to want a common agricultural policy. I invited representatives of 20 Member States to Vienna in October last year; all 20 countries were high-ranking, most with ministers. It was about the dairy sector, to find answers to the dairy sector, but also answers to the common agricultural policy, especially with our Eastern European neighbours. We agree that we still want to have a common agricultural policy, that the agricultural budget must also be sufficiently paid, that there should not be any reductions, but that we do not want renationalization, because there would be unnecessary competition to the detriment of farmers. We want a strong first and second pillar, and we also want the ecologically sustainable path of our agriculture to continue. Mrs Binder-Maier, you asked me, so I am also referring to this issue. That is why the assessment law is concerned, because the larger farmers are nevertheless in the accounting obligation. [[the speaker leaves in such a pass]] Some of them are rather disturbing things that stand in there. I'd like to read this to you: One knows today that even when smoking a cigarette, the child's heart beats by a quarter faster than before, that the child gets little oxygen that reduces the intake of nutrients through the mother's blood. Babies of mothers who smoke actively or passively during pregnancy are often softer, weaker than children of non-smokers. And: Passive smoking can cause sudden infant death. I think there's nothing more terrible than the fear of sudden infant death. I think all parents know this: the fear of looking into the cradle – and the child is no longer alive! And then you read in this mother-child pass, passive smoking increases the risk of sudden infant death. – How am I now a waitress in a so-called conversion facility? However, these pollutant limit values only affect the pollutants created by the work itself, i.e. the solvent when painting, the glue in the carpenter, also the noise. Smoking, however, is not included, smoking is completely irrelevant. He who smokes is not counted there. I also asked the Minister for Health, and what was the answer? No matter where you look at it, whether it's New York or Rome, California or Scotland, it's proven that way everywhere. What does the law say? – The law was passed in 2008 by 125 votes of the Social Democrats, the People's Party and the Greens; 23 Members of this House voted against. ==References====External links== The last provisions of this Federal Law, the Tobacco Act, only enter into force on July 1, 2009. ==References== What were the central innovations of this federal law? – The very central innovation was that smoking is also banned in the gastronomy, and there is a fundamental ban on smoking in the Austrian gastronomy. – However, there are exceptions, and these exceptions are – I admit – very broad, but will become less and less after a transitional period. With the exception of this provision, holdings with less than 50 m2 of space are holdings. There the host can decide whether to allow smoking or not. Farms between 50 and 80 m2 are covered by the transitional scheme, but with 1. ==References== In these places you can smoke if there is a separation. In locals with over 80 m2 of space, however, you need clearly separate smoking rooms. It is also clear that tobacco advertising is no longer permitted in Austria. These are the essential contents of the Tobacco Act – and this has led to the fact that smoking has actually been reduced in Austria. Smoking, ladies and gentlemen, is an emotional issue that directly affects people in their lives. A third observation on Mr Pirklhuber's speech: These deceptions, which he has presented, are already prohibited. There is a clear record of action in § 2 and § 5 of the Food Safety and Consumer Protection Act, according to which an error in the presentation is generally prohibited. Food supervision is the responsibility of the institutions of the federal states. It is, for example, Mr Pirklhuber, at Mr Anschober in Upper Austria that he is causing such checks. Dr. Pirklhuber.]] I have wondered about Mr Anschober's statements in the public, which apparently was not aware of the fact that there is already a ban in the LMSVG and that has publicly asked the National Council to establish such a scheme. I'll hold you once again. The control of the existing provisions on food is solely on the food supervision of the federal states, regardless of whether this is Styria, Salzburg, Burgenland or Vienna. In the context of the indirect federal administration, the supervision of foodstuffs has to be subject to the control of these legal provisions. Now I will come back to the text of the motion for a resolution. The Greens demand in your application a change to the AMA law – I may explain it, Mr Pirklhuber, because that was not done by you! – by means of § 3 para. 1 Z 2 a supplement should be made that it should also be the task of the AMA to ensure free of genetic engineering production. Of course, we can discuss this. I personally believe that it would be a sovereign task and not the task of AMA to ensure free of genetic engineering production. This, Mr Pirklhuber, is linked to the Commission statement that has been in place recently. The Commission intends to propose that Member States will be able to decide themselves on the cultivation of genetically modified organisms in the future. What does that mean? – It will be the responsibility of the Minister of Health and the Minister of Agriculture to ensure free production of genetic engineering. But it will not be the AMA's task to regulate that. We therefore see no reason to vote in favour of this request at present.

Mr Maier also mentioned with the last sentence what Mr Pirklhuber meant by his request: first of all, that the AMA should be transferred to the AMA free of genetic engineering as a task in its own field of activity, namely through this Section 3 para. 1 Z 2 in the AMA Act. Secondly, even if they are not marketed as organic products, they are intended to be used exclusively for marketing promotion measures for organic products. This is regulated by § 21j para. 2 of the AMA Act. I should like to point out that in the case of non-genotechnic production: This possibility is already covered in the AMA Act by § 21a, even if it is not explicitly mentioned. It is said in concrete terms: § 21a para. 1 Z 2 allows the use of the agricultural marketing contribution to promote general measures for improving and ensuring quality in these products and to provide information relevant to consumers regarding quality, aspects of consumer protection and animal welfare and other product characteristics. Now you could say, of course, if you mention it, then actually all that is not mentioned is not possible. That could be a shot at the back. That is why, I believe, it is not absolutely appropriate to do so now. The AMA Quality Guidelines for the AMA Quality Seal already contain the freedom of genetic engineering as a voluntary module. And I would like to point this out again: If the AMA seal is on the package, you can probably assume that the raw material actually comes to 100 percent from Austria, except for mixed products, if, for example, it is contained in yogurt vanilla or in the sausage pepper that comes from elsewhere. But when it comes to meat and milk, there is 100 percent Austria inside. As far as the second point is concerned, this commitment is intended for the promotion of organic production: Mr Pirklhuber says that only the milk actually used as organic milk is used as the basis for the allocation of the AMA marketing agents. This is not true because the entire biomilk production has been used for this since 2008. Another thing: I believe that it would not necessarily be reasonable to split everything exactly and split it into separate parts. I can say now I've got organic. Then I can distinguish with organic, I make separate advertising for milk, for meat separate advertising, for fruit and for vegetables and what else there are products, a separate rail in advertising. Now I stay with the milk – what do I do? Do I make a separate advertising track for milk produced from silofood and a second advertising rail for milk produced from silo-free feeding? Then I still have to distinguish: Do I make a separate advertising track for hay milk, which is organic, and for hay milk that is not organic, a separate advertising rail? I believe that this is not quite a goal, but it should be left to the horizontal approach, since it will certainly be better to drive in the future. In conclusion: I believe that this proposal is overall superfluous and therefore we do not need the deadline proposal either. I believe that this request by Mr Pirklhuber would bring overall higher costs and lower efficiency. That is why it would not be right to agree. We will therefore not give our approval to the deadline request in this case.

Our demands once again: a new seal of quality law, with which the seal of quality is recognised by the state, with clear criteria as to origin as regards animal welfare as regards ecological criteria. And: The AMA's bio-marketing contributions must only benefit organic farmers. Austrian products have a high priority among Austrians. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] By the way, I believe that Austria needs an independent, independent and committed Ministry of the Environment – in addition to the labelling of food.

That is why there has been a ban on ivory trade since 1990, but it has already been mentioned, at this conference, two countries, Tanzania and Zambia, have now requested that the protection status of elephants be loosened, namely that Annex I be lowered to Annex II, which is a legalisation of ivory exports. This is contrary to the ivory ban, and it would also have been an affront to all other African countries that have approached other countries, including the European Union, and have asked for massive support for these applications. Only once again to illustrate: In Tanzania there have been 34 000 elephants less in the last three years, in Zambia the stock has fallen from 160 000 to 26 000, i.e. 84 percent, and both countries have not been able to provide the required evidence that the ivory traded there is also from legal sources. Legal sources mean that elephants have died on natural meadow. Unfortunately, illegal hunting, wild animals play a major role in this. That is why we have tabled a request - precisely before the decision - which was discussed in the Committee on the Environment last week, whether this protection status is loosened or not. The decision took place last week. The essential point was that protection status should not be reduced. That is why we have also insisted massively on this request and have not agreed to the request of the coalition parties with the other opposition parties, because it is precisely on the essential point that the protection status is not loosened. Everything else would have been just eye-removing and would have enabled this relaxation. But what you have represented there, Minister of Agriculture, is a question from us, where it was clearly said that this was the Austrian position, namely the position of the official who represented us there. It says: “... In particular, elephants, lions, rhinos and leopards are treated by animal protection organisations such as sacred cows, with commercial use only possible effective protection for these species in politically relative stable southern Africa. Sustainable trophy hunting, in addition to phototourism and biotope protection, is often inextricable.” So you have to explain the term “sustainable trophy hunt. ” This is already a contradiction in itself, and I think that it is clear which lobbys you actually represent – animal welfare and species protection certainly not. The effect was that the peso immediately lost 80 percent of its value to the dollar and that the Argentinean citizens in Buenos Aires stood in front of the banks and wanted to pick up their last parlors. Ladies and gentlemen! The southern European countries, the so-called PIGS – Portugal, Italy – but I would also like to add Ireland as “I” – are standing here today, where Argentina was established eight years ago. The first state with massive problems is Greece now, and the others too will be added. The criticisms of this monetary union have already said ten years ago that it cannot work if countries with different economic strength, different economic performance and objectives, such as the southern European states and, on the other hand, the Central and Northern European countries join together into a monetary union. There's going to be, there's got to be fractions, and they're here now. That is why I believe our motion for a resolution that we can allow a separation – I say – of these southern currencies to be good and supportive. We must give these southern European countries the opportunity to withdraw from the euro, because, ladies and gentlemen, this will not work from today to tomorrow. They have to print money notes; they have to distribute the money; they have to make calculations. This cannot be achieved from today to tomorrow, especially since the southern Europeans are connected to the euro in a structurally uniform currency. The Argentines still had their peso, they still had their paper and their coin money. That's not what the South Europeans have. That's why you have to take care of yourself. The other solution – Mr President, Mr President-in-Office of the Council – is not going to be, even because of speculations, to keep these countries inside – requires enormous costs. A European Monetary Fund is addressed. You know exactly, Mr President, what this means. This is another pot of redistribution from Northern and Central Europe to Southern Europe. When you consider that Greece alone needs €57 billion this year to guarantee its state stability – by summer should be given €25 billion – I wonder what will happen if Portugal comes and needs €100 billion, Spain €200 billion, Italy €500 billion. Who's to pay for all this, ladies and gentlemen? Italy will be the biggest problem. Italy has €1,900 billion of public debt. Italy pays €100 billion in interest. If the Italians decouple themselves to an Italian euro or again the lira, the problem will be that the interest payments will immediately jump at a huge amount, double. That's where you have to give help. We need to remember now that time is ripe – that's it now! – that the southern European States will again be able to withdraw into their own currencies, into their own monetary union if it is to be. Then they also have the possibility of the exchange rate mechanism. But we will have to help them deal with interest rate problems. Support You have our request so that we can think about it in good time!
